## C. During her life (1873 to her death, 30 September 1897)

### M-079 — The aunt's vow to St Francis de Sales, January–February 1873
- Date: Feb 1873
- Status label: family report or tradition; popular report
- What was reported: Zélie's sister Sr Marie-Dosithée, at the Visitation of Le Mans, prayed to St Francis de Sales and vowed that if the child lived she would bear his name, Françoise. The Superior told the nuns to write at once. The aunt wrote that, if Zélie refused, the saint was free to take the child back, and she need only "have a coffin made". Zélie refused, saying the child had improved before the prayers.
- The person helped: baby Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: Zélie, writing to Isidore on 1 Mar 1873; the aunt's letter is not itself preserved in my sources
- Status in the sources: popular tradition / family letter
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): none stated beyond the illness in M-LF-001
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): The child was in fact baptised "Marie-Françoise-Thérèse" (baptism record, LAV p.461); Zélie's refusal concerned the daily name. Zélie thought the letter would make people believe she had lost her head.
- Note: Related to M-080 (the illness of March 1873). Not counted twice: it is a separate claim, that the aunt's prayers and vow to St Francis de Sales saved the child.
- Source entries: M-LF-002
- Sources cited: CF 87; F-LF-042; rights: public-domain letter, our English

### M-080 — The baby Thérèse near death, cured in the wet nurse's lap (Alençon, March 1873)
- Date: Mar 1873
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; family report or tradition
- What was reported: At about nine weeks old (Céline: "a few weeks", elsewhere "a few months") Thérèse had a weak intestinal illness that Zélie feared was the illness that had killed two little brothers. Two doctors gave her up (Céline). Dr Belloc examined her on the Monday evening and said only the breast could save her. The wet nurse Rose Taillé of Semallé reached the house at 10.30 a.m. Zélie prayed on her knees at the feet of St Joseph in her room. When she came down she saw the baby "nursing with all her heart". At about 1 p.m. the baby brought up some mouthfuls and "fell as if dead" on the nurse. Five people stood round, a workwoman wept, and no breath could be seen. After a quarter of an hour the baby opened her eyes and smiled: "From that moment she was completely cured." Céline (family tradition; she was 3–4 at the time) swore the cure came through St Joseph's intercession.
- The person helped: [LF-001] Thérèse Martin, about nine weeks old, with a weak intestinal illness that Zélie feared was the illness of her dead children [EW-004] Thérèse, infant
- Witnesses and reporter: [LF-001] Zélie (writer, March 1873, to Mme Guérin); "five around her" (the nurse, a workwoman and others, unnamed); the doctor M. Belloc gave the advice [EW-004] Céline, family tradition (she was 3–4)
- Status in the sources: [LF-001] recorded by the family (a letter). Zélie does not call it a miracle. It is not one of the cases in the Cause. [EW-004] sworn testimony, family tradition; not an approved miracle
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): [LF-001] Dr Belloc examined the child on the Monday evening and prescribed rice-water and lime-water in milk twice a day, and said that only the breast could save her. She was fed by the wet nurse; the baby brought up some mouthfuls after feeding. No other treatment is named. [EW-004] enteritis; wet-nursing followed on the doctor's advice [633]
- Where the sources differ: Zélie does not call it a miracle, and it is not one of the cases in the Cause. CF 87 (1 Mar 1873) says the child had improved before anyone prayed to St Francis de Sales (the aunt's vow, M-079). Céline gives "a few months" at PA 8 [668] and "a few weeks" at [776]. Laveille smooths the letters.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [LF-001] CF 87 (1 Mar 1873) says the child had improved before anyone prayed to St Francis de Sales (M-LF-002). Laveille smooths the letters. [EW-004] Céline gives "a few months" at [668] and "a few weeks" at [776].
- Source entries: M-LF-001, M-EW-004
- Sources cited: [LF-001] CF 89 (raw-notes/letters-text/de-mme-martin-a-mme-guerin-cf-89-mars-1873.txt; https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/de-mme-martin-a-mme-guerin-cf-89-mars-1873/); LAV p.48–49; F-LF-041, F-LF-043; rights: public-domain letter, our English [EW-004] PA 8, [668], Session 29; [776], Session 37

### M-081 — The toddler found asleep on a chair (Alençon, 1874)
- Date: c. 1874
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; family report or tradition
- What was reported: Zélie forgot to push the cradle against her bed (Céline: she left the child in the big bed and forgot to bring the cradle close to stop her falling). She went to the 5.30 a.m. Mass. On her return she found the child's place empty, then found the toddler asleep sitting on a high chair by the bed, head on the bolster, unhurt. She could not understand how the child got onto the chair. Zélie credited her guardian angel and the souls in purgatory; Marie says the mother saw God's intervention, and that the child had fallen from her cradle and could not have climbed the chair herself.
- The person helped: [LF-021] Thérèse, about 18 months old [EW-005] Thérèse, about 18 months (Marie)
- Witnesses and reporter: [LF-021] Zélie (unpublished letter, undated in Laveille) [EW-005] Zélie Martin (not a witness here); reported by Marie and Céline from family tradition
- Status in the sources: [LF-021] recorded by the family [EW-005] sworn testimony at the Apostolic Process (family tradition, not eyewitness); not an approved miracle
- Where the sources differ: The two sisters disagree on whether Thérèse was in a cradle or in the mother's bed. Marie says about 18 months old and attributes it to her guardian angel. Not one of the miracles of the Cause.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [LF-021] Not one of the miracles of the Cause. [EW-005] The two sisters disagree on whether she was in a cradle or in the mother's bed. Marie attributes it to her guardian angel.
- Source entries: M-LF-021, M-EW-005
- Sources cited: [LF-021] LAV p.56–57; F-LF-055 [EW-005] PA 7, [560], Session 22; PA 8, [776]–[777], Session 37

### M-082 — Vision of a veiled, bent man like her father (Les Buissonnets, c. 1879–80)
- Date: c. 1879
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; family report or tradition; her own report
- What was reported: At about six or seven, alone at a window of Les Buissonnets on a sunny afternoon (about 2 p.m.), while her father was away at Alençon, Thérèse saw in the garden (about 20 metres away) a man with her father's height and walk but much more stooped, as if by age, his head covered with a kind of apron or veil of no clear colour (Marie: dark cloth; Céline: thick veil). He walked toward a grove and vanished. She called "Papa, papa". Marie and Pauline ran from the next room, searched the walled garden and found no trace (the maid Victoire denied hiding under her apron). They told her not to think or speak of it. She stayed convinced it announced a trial. In 1889, at the start of his cerebral paralysis, Louis often covered his head with a handkerchief. The family and Thérèse read the vision as a foreseeing of his illness.
- The person helped: [OW-01] none; a sign concerning Louis Martin [EW-007] none (premonition) [EW-008] none (prophecy)
- Witnesses and reporter: [OW-01] Thérèse alone saw it; Marie (in the next room) and Pauline heard her call and searched with her. Reported by Thérèse in Ms A, 1895 [EW-007] Mère Agnès, present right after; Marie [EW-008] Marie heard the cry; Céline also testifies to the handkerchief [EW-009] Marie (heard the cry, present); Céline (absent, heard it from her sisters)
- Status in the sources: [OW-01] her own report; not investigated [EW-007] sworn testimony at the Ordinary Process; not an approved miracle [EW-008] sworn testimony; interpretation by the family [EW-009] sworn testimony; treated as prophetic vision; not a miracle
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): [EW-007] Pauline says Thérèse was calm, balanced, "not imaginative"; the vision came three years before her childhood illness. [EW-008] Louis's "paralysie cérébrale" [EW-009] Louis's cerebral paralysis, 1889
- Where the sources differ: Timing of the interpretation: Thérèse and Marie read it 14 years later after the illness (OW-01, Ms A); Marie says the meaning was understood when Thérèse retold it on a recreation day in Carmel; Céline says it was revealed only after their father's death (1894). Pauline says Thérèse believed from the start it foretold something painful. The judge asked whether the sad meaning was only claimed after the illness. Marie dates it 1879, "ten years before the event"; Pauline says it came three years before the childhood illness.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [OW-01] Marie's own account belongs to other parts. The interpretation came 14 years later, after the father's illness. [EW-007] Told in MS A. The judge asked whether the sad meaning was only claimed after the father's illness; Pauline said Thérèse believed from the start it foretold something painful. [EW-008] Marie dates the vision 1879, "ten years before the event". [EW-009] Marie says the meaning was understood when Thérèse retold it on a recreation day in Carmel; Céline says it was revealed only after their father's death (1894). Marie: "dark cloth"; Céline: "thick veil".
- Source entries: M-OW-01, M-EW-007, M-EW-008, M-EW-009
- Sources cited: [OW-01] Ms A 19v–21r (F-OW-028); public-domain [EW-007] PO 1, f. 233r–v, Session 17, 31 Aug 1910 [EW-008] PO 3, f. 322v–323r; PO 4, f. 394v–395r [EW-009] PA 7, [604]–[605], Session 25; PA 8, [778], Session 37

### M-083 — The cure of Thérèse by the smile of the Virgin (Les Buissonnets, Pentecost, 13 May 1883)
- Date: 13 May 1883
- Status label: sworn at the Church process (Marie, Céline and Pauline); not one of the miracles chosen for the decrees; the statue is venerated as the "Vierge du Sourire"
- What was reported: Thérèse, aged 10, was ill from March 1883 (constant headache from late 1882, a trembling fit at Easter). She had terrors, hallucinations, falls from the bed, head-banging, loss of voice and delirium-like states in which she did not know her sisters. A novena of Masses at Notre-Dame des Victoires, paid by her father, was under way. On Sunday 13 May, in Marie's room, she called "Mama" without recognizing Marie. Marie, Léonie and Céline knelt and prayed before the statue of the Virgin; at Marie's third prayer Thérèse stared at the statue with a radiant, ecstatic look for four or five minutes and two large tears fell. Thérèse writes: "All at once the Blessed Virgin seemed to me beautiful... what went to the depths of my soul was the 'ravishing smile of the Blessed Virgin'... all my pains vanished." She then knew Marie without effort and told her she had seen the Virgin (she wept "because I could not see her any more"). From then no trace of illness was seen and she resumed her normal life the next day. Louis wrote to Nogrix that she was "tout à fait guérie". In 1897, in the infirmary, she said of the same statue: "today it is the statue; before, you know it wasn't the statue".
- The person helped: [OW-02] Thérèse Martin, aged 10 [LF-003] Thérèse Martin, aged 10, ill since March 1883 [EW-011, EW-012] Thérèse, aged 10 [EW-013] Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: [OW-02] Marie, Léonie, Céline present; Thérèse's account in Ms A (1895); she told Marie at once; the Carmelites questioned her soon after [LF-003] Marie (Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur), Léonie and Céline in the room; Thérèse wrote it in Ms A (27r–31v); Marie deposed at the Process (Summarium 1919 p.416). Louis wrote to Nogrix (1883) that she was "tout à fait guérie". [EW-011] Marie (eyewitness); Léonie and Céline present [EW-012] Marie (present, led the prayer); Céline (present, then 13–14: "I had no doubt the Virgin had appeared to her"); Dr Notta, who said "not hysteria" [EW-013] Mère Agnès reports; she was in Carmel and knew it from the parlour and from Thérèse
- Status in the sources: [OW-02] family and Carmel tradition; the statue is venerated as the "Vierge du Sourire"; not an approved miracle [LF-003] investigated by the Church (in the process testimony); not one of the miracles chosen for the decrees [EW-011] sworn testimony at the Ordinary Process; not an approved canonization miracle [EW-012] sworn testimony at the Apostolic Process; not one of the approved canonisation miracles [EW-013] sworn testimony; not an approved miracle
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): [OW-02] constant headache from late 1882; trembling fit at Easter 1883; treatment with blankets and hot bottles; Dr Notta consulted; she refused "very simple remedies" from fear [LF-003] Dr Notta treated her and said "Science is powerless in face of these phenomena; nothing can be done." Symptoms in F-LF-139. No treatment is named. [EW-011] hallucinations, falls from bed, head-banging, loss of voice, showers prescribed; doctor said "science is powerless" [EW-012] daily showers prescribed; hallucinations; head-banging; muteness; about six weeks; no relapse; "science powerless" [EW-013] "passing" illness, left no trace
- Where the sources differ: Date: the usual date is 13 May 1883 (Pentecost); Céline and Pauline give 10 May. What she saw: Thérèse (Ms A) does not say the statue moved ("seemed to me"); Pauline reports she saw the statue "come alive, approach and smile"; Laveille says she saw it "become animated". Manner of cure: "not all at once, but gently" (Ms A) against instant and permanent (Marie, Céline). Cause of the illness: Thérèse, Marie, Céline and Laveille call it diabolic; Céline notes religious objects never frightened her, unlike usual demonic cases, and that grief at losing her "mothers" to Carmel may have contributed. Dr Notta said "not hysteria" (Céline) and "Science is powerless in face of these phenomena" (Marie, Laveille). Later doubt: she feared she had "lied" and for four years suspected she had made the illness on purpose (F-OW-041, F-OW-042); the nuns' questions afterwards troubled her (F-LF-143). She never used the word "miracle". The illness lasted about six weeks. Pauline defers to her sisters for details.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [OW-02] She later feared she had "lied" about it and for four years suspected she had made herself ill on purpose (F-OW-041, F-OW-042). She does not say the statue moved. She blamed the illness on the devil. Dr Notta called the illness "very grave" and unknown in so young a child; no diagnosis is given in her text. [LF-003] The nuns' questions afterwards troubled Thérèse (F-LF-143). She never used the word "miracle". Laveille ascribes the illness to the demon; no diagnosis is given; the Carmel has a psychiatrist's page (not read). [EW-011] Céline dates the cure 10 May (usual date 13 May 1883). Marie calls the illness diabolical; Céline notes religious objects never frightened her, unlike usual demonic cases. [EW-012] Marie and Céline both call the illness diabolic in origin. Céline suggests grief at losing her "mothers" to Carmel may have contributed. [EW-013] Pauline gives the date as 10 May 1883; the usual date is 13 May 1883 (Pentecost). Pauline defers to her sisters for details. In the infirmary in 1897 Pauline placed the same statue near her; Thérèse said it never seemed so beautiful, "but today it's the statue; before, you know it wasn't the statue" (f. 234v).
- Source entries: M-OW-02, M-LF-003, M-EW-011, M-EW-012, M-EW-013
- Sources cited: [OW-02] Ms A 27r–31r, 56v–57r, 86r (F-OW-038 to F-OW-042, F-OW-070); public-domain [LF-003] LAV p.129–132; Carmel page https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/la-vie-de-sainte-therese-de-lisieux/la-maladie-de-therese-a-10-ans/ (date); letter to Nogrix (raw-notes/letters-text/de-louis-martin-a-m-francois-julien-nogrix-1883.txt); F-LF-138 to F-LF-143, F-LF-147; rights: public-domain and unknown [EW-011] PO 3, f. 311v–312r and 323r, S23 and S25 [EW-012] PA 7, [562]–[565], [604], [615], Sessions 22, 25, 26; PA 8, [637]–[638], [777], Sessions 27, 37 [EW-013] PO 1, f. 234r–v, Session 17, 31 Aug 1910

### M-084 — Candles to the Virgin and the awaited telegram
- Date: 2 Oct 1886
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: Thérèse lit a candle to the Virgin every day and prayed hard that Marie would learn in time to meet "le Père" at Dover. A telegram then came, which she took to mean Marie had met him.
- The person helped: Marie Martin (her sister) and the family.
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse, writing the same evening.
- Status in the sources: her own report (an answered prayer, in her view).
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Céline also sent letters to Dover and Calais, so ordinary means were used too.
- Source entries: M-OW-27
- Sources cited: LT 21 (to Marie, 2 Oct 1886), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-21-a-marie-2-octobre-1886/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-085 — Pranzini's "sign", 31 Aug 1887
- Date: 31 Aug 1887
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: She prayed and had a Mass said for the murderer Henri Pranzini and asked God for "a sign" of repentance. He did not confess, but on the scaffold he took the priest's crucifix and kissed the wounds three times (reported in *La Croix*, read by her the next day).
- The person helped: Henri Pranzini, executed in Paris
- Witnesses and reporter: newspaper report; Thérèse's account (Ms A, 1895); Céline shared the secret and the prayer
- Status in the sources: her own report of an answered prayer
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): The press account is outside this source family (check the *La Croix* issue of 1 Sep 1887 in other parts).
- Source entries: M-OW-03
- Sources cited: Ms A 45v–46v (F-OW-064); public-domain

### M-086 — Her uncle's "inclination of heart", Oct 1887
- Date: Oct 1887
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: Her uncle said it would take "a miracle" to make him consent to her entering at 15. After three days of spiritual darkness and rain, he called her in and said he had asked God only for "a simple inclination of heart" and had received it. She writes: "for me the miracle was granted."
- The person helped: Thérèse (her vocation)
- Witnesses and reporter: Isidore Guérin; Thérèse's account
- Status in the sources: her own interpretation
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): A change of mind, not a physical event. Listed because she calls it a miracle.
- Source entries: M-OW-04
- Sources cited: Ms A 50v–51v (F-OW-065); public-domain

### M-087 — Frequent dreams of her family
- Date: Nov 1887
- Status label: her own report
- Flag: no extraordinary claim, or no outcome: listed only because the source reports it
- What was reported: In Italy she thought of her family "so often that I dream of it at night". In Carmel: "it is unbelievable how often I dream of" her cousin Jeanne.
- The person helped: none.
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse.
- Status in the sources: her own report of ordinary dreams.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): No claim of anything extraordinary; listed only because the rules ask for dreams.
- Source entries: M-OW-30
- Sources cited: LT 37 (to Marie Guérin, 25 Nov 1887), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-37-a-marie-guerin-25-novembre-1887/ ; LT 50 (to Marie Guérin, 13 May 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-50-a-marie-guerin-13-mai-1888/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-088 — The Child Jesus's "yes" for Christmas
- Date: Dec 1887
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: Thérèse wrote that the Child Jesus "made me feel so clearly that he wanted me at Christmas". After the bishop sent her New Year gifts, she wrote: "So the Child Jesus did not deceive me! He said yes to me from his cradle."
- The person helped: Thérèse.
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse (letters to the bishop).
- Status in the sources: her own report of an inner conviction; the bishop's positive answer is the outward event.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): She did not in fact enter at Christmas; entry was on 9 April 1888 (baseline; Ms A says the prioress delayed it until after Lent). LT 40 is an unfinished draft.
- Source entries: M-OW-28
- Sources cited: LT 38 (to Mgr Hugonin, 3–8 Dec 1887), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-38-a-mgr-hugonin-3-8-decembre-1887/ ; LT 40 (to Mgr Hugonin, early Jan 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-40-a-mgr-hugonin-debut-janvier-1888/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-089 — Wild flowers left on the window (Carmel, summer 1888)
- Date: Jul 1888
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; personal report or belief of the witness
- What was reported: In her first summer in Carmel Thérèse grieved that she would never again see cornflowers, daisies, poppies, oats or wheat, and told no one. The outside portress (extern sister) then found on her window-sill, left by an unknown hand, a fine sheaf of field flowers with exactly those flowers and ears. It was passed inside to the prioress, who did not know Thérèse's wish and gave it to her for the statue of the Child Jesus. From then on she never lacked wild flowers.
- The person helped: [EW-016, EW-017] Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: [EW-016] Céline (then outside; hearsay) [EW-017] Thérèse told Céline
- Status in the sources: [EW-016] sworn testimony; "a delicacy of heaven", not a miracle [EW-017] private memoir; not a process miracle
- Where the sources differ: Céline (Apostolic Process, hearsay: she was outside) calls it "a delicacy of heaven", not a miracle. In the private memoir Céline says Thérèse told her. An anonymous gift is a natural explanation; Céline presents it as Jesus providing.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [EW-017] an anonymous gift is a natural explanation; Céline presents it as Jesus providing.
- Source entries: M-EW-016, M-EW-017
- Sources cited: [EW-016] PA 8, [777]–[778], Session 37 [EW-017] CS §III "Des fleurs pour la statue de l'Enfant Jésus"

### M-090 — Dream that Pauline would soon die
- Date: 11 Jul 1888
- Status label: her own report
- Flag: no extraordinary claim, or no outcome: listed only because the source reports it
- What was reported: "I dreamed that the lamb would soon fly away to its homeland." In Jan 1889 she again feared Pauline (who had a bad headache) would die.
- The person helped: none (a fear).
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse.
- Status in the sources: her own report of a dream.
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): a headache (LT 78).
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): The dream was not fulfilled; Pauline lived until 1951.
- Source entries: M-OW-29
- Sources cited: LT 56 (to Sr Agnès, 11 Jul 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-56-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-11-juillet-1888/ ; LT 78, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-78-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-8-janvier-1889/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-091 — M. David's holy death as an answer to Mme Guérin's prayers
- Date: 22 Aug 1888
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: Thérèse thanks God for "the great grace" given to the dying M. David ("what dispositions to appear before him") and says God answered all the prayers her aunt made "to give him the soul of your dear relative".
- The person helped: M. David (relative of Mme Guérin).
- Witnesses and reporter: Mme Guérin (letter to the Carmel, not in this file); Thérèse's replies.
- Status in the sources: her own interpretation of a report from her aunt.
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): he was dying; he died on 22 Aug 1888 (news came "hier soir" before 23 Aug).
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): The letters do not say what M. David's earlier religious state was.
- Source entries: M-OW-31
- Sources cited: LT 59 (22 Aug 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-59-a-m-guerin-22-aout-1888/ ; LT 60 (23 Aug 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-60-a-mme-guerin-23-aout-1888/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-092 — Her father's recovery in autumn 1888 after the Carmel's prayers
- Date: 15 Nov 1888
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: "You were very ill. The whole Carmel was in prayer, and so God at last heard its sighs and gave me back my King." To her old teacher: "Jesus deigned to restore him for the time of my clothing."
- The person helped: Louis Martin.
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse; the Carmel community (praying).
- Status in the sources: her own report of an answered prayer.
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): "très malade"; no diagnosis.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): On 25 Nov (LT 68) she says he is not yet "entirely cured". His confinement at Caen followed in Feb 1889 (LT 82).
- Source entries: M-OW-32
- Sources cited: LT 66 (15 Nov 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-66-a-m-martin-15-novembre-1888/ ; LT 70 (early Dec 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-70-a-mere-saint-placide-debut-decembre-1888/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-093 — Snow on her clothing day (Carmel, 10 January 1889)
- Date: 10 Jan 1889
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine; her own report; popular report
- What was reported: Thérèse always wished for snow on her clothing day. The day was extremely mild and rainy (fine rain the day before and that morning). After the ceremony in the church, as she entered the cloister, she saw the quadrangle or courtyard covered with snow: "What tenderness on the part of Jesus!" (she calls it a "delicacy of Jesus"). "The whole town was astonished"; the community and others spoke of "the little miracle".
- The person helped: [LF-005] Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: [OW-05] the community and town (per her); Thérèse's account [LF-005] Thérèse (HA ch. vii p.124); the community
- Status in the sources: [OW-05] her own report; she calls it a "delicacy of Jesus" [LF-005] popular tradition / recorded by the monastery
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): [OW-05] mild temperature and fine rain the day before and that morning (her words)
- Where the sources differ: Weather records for Lisieux, 10 Jan 1889, were not checked. Laveille says the weather was mild until the ceremony.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [OW-05] Weather records for Lisieux, 10 Jan 1889, not checked. [LF-005] Laveille says the weather that day was mild until the ceremony. No weather record was checked.
- Source entries: M-OW-05, M-LF-005
- Sources cited: [OW-05] Ms A 72r–72v (F-OW-086); public-domain [LF-005] LAV p.214–215; F-LF-270; rights: public-domain

### M-094 — Dream of consoling Marie Guérin; hope of a cure from the Virgin
- Date: 30 May 1889
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: Before receiving Marie Guérin's letter, Thérèse "sensed" her anguish; that night she dreamed she was trying to console her, without success. She hoped the Virgin would "cure" her cousin (of scruples) on the last day of May, and suggested a candle at Notre-Dame-des-Victoires.
- The person helped: Marie Guérin.
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse.
- Status in the sources: her own report of a premonition-like feeling and a dream; a hoped-for grace.
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): scruples (fear of sin), missed Communions.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): LT 93 (July 1889) shows the scruples were still present in July; no cure is reported in these letters.
- Source entries: M-OW-33
- Sources cited: LT 92 (30 May 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-92-a-marie-guerin-30-mai-1889/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-095 — Marcelline's vocation obstacles removed (1889)
- Date: 1889
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; personal report or belief of the witness
- What was reported: Marcelline faced great outside obstacles and painful scruples before entering religion. She asked the prayers of Thérèse, who had just taken the habit. Quickly all difficulties were smoothed out.
- The person helped: Marcelline Husé
- Witnesses and reporter: Marcelline
- Status in the sources: sworn testimony at the Apostolic Process; a personal grace, not a miracle claim
- Source entries: M-EW-019
- Sources cited: PA-22 [1338–1339]

### M-096 — Léonie hopes for a cure at Tours (Holy Face) or Lourdes
- Date: May 1890
- Status label: a hope reported second-hand; no outcome in these letters
- Flag: no extraordinary claim, or no outcome: listed only because the source reports it
- What was reported: Léonie, suffering from a swelling her uncle thought dangerous, hoped to be cured "at the Holy Face or at Lourdes" and planned to go into the pool.
- The person helped: Léonie Martin.
- Witnesses and reporter: Céline (told the Carmel in the parlour); Thérèse (letter).
- Status in the sources: a hope, reported second-hand; no outcome in these letters.
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): "c'est enflé tout autour"; the uncle (a pharmacist) judged it dangerous.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Thérèse herself declined any wish for Lourdes "ecstasies" (LT 106).
- Source entries: M-OW-34
- Sources cited: LT 104 (fragments, 5–6 May 1890), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-104-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-fragments-5-6-mai-1890/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-097 — Mère Geneviève's word during Louis's illness, "verified on the following day"
- Date: c. 1890
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine
- What was reported: When Louis's state caused his daughters "the most poignant anguish", the invalid foundress Mother Geneviève sent for Thérèse and her sisters and calmed them by repeating "words Our Lord had spoken to her", which "were verified on the following day". The words are not given.
- The person helped: the Martin sisters
- Witnesses and reporter: the Carmel biography of Mère Geneviève (FND p.42; LAV p.203 fn., citing the 1909 biography p.89)
- Status in the sources: recorded by the monastery
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): No date, no wording, no witness named.
- Source entries: M-LF-004
- Sources cited: FND p.42; LAV p.203 fn.; rights: public-domain

### M-098 — The Céline flower blooming a month early
- Date: Oct 1891
- Status label: a natural event read as a sign; she does not call it a miracle
- Flag: no extraordinary claim, or no outcome: listed only because the source reports it
- What was reported: After the harshest winter, the "Céline" flower bloomed a month early. She read it as a sign about Céline's soul.
- The person helped: Céline (symbolically)
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse, 20 Oct 1891
- Status in the sources: a natural event read as a sign. She does not call it a miracle.
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): a harsh winter delaying other flowers
- Source entries: M-OW-26
- Sources cited: LT 132, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-132-a-celine-20-octobre-1891/ ; public-domain

### M-099 — Céline consoled and advised from a distance after a short parlour visit (1888–94)
- Date: c. 1891
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: When a parlour visit ended too soon, or when Thérèse failed to comfort Céline, she asked Jesus to tell Céline what she had wished to say, and to console her and make her understand certain named things. At the next parlour visit Céline's own "good inspirations" and confidences matched exactly the advice or lights Thérèse had intended. Thérèse said her trust was never disappointed.
- The person helped: [EW-015] Céline [EW-018] Céline Martin
- Witnesses and reporter: [EW-015] Céline [EW-018] Mère Agnès, told by Thérèse
- Status in the sources: [EW-015] sworn testimony; not a miracle [EW-018] sworn testimony; not an approved miracle
- Where the sources differ: Céline gives it first-hand (Apostolic Process, PA 8 [682]–[683]); Pauline gives it second-hand from Thérèse (PO 1, f. 173v–174r). No independent check. A related letter of July 1893: M-104.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [EW-015] Only Céline's account. [EW-018] Second-hand; no independent check.
- Source entries: M-EW-015, M-EW-018
- Sources cited: [EW-015] PA 8, [682]–[683], Session 30 [EW-018] PO 1, f. 173v–174r, Session 9, 19 Aug 1910

### M-100 — Mystical states: transports of love, eight days "outside her body", flights of the spirit
- Date: c. 1892
- Status label: sworn at the Church process (Pauline); not an approved miracle; an inner experience
- What was reported: Before Carmel she had "transports of love" and told God she would even go to hell so that He would be loved there. In her noviciate, for nearly eight days she felt separated from her body, doing refectory work "as if with a borrowed body". Several times in the garden at the evening great silence she felt "flights of the spirit" (St Teresa's term). Reported by Mère Agnès from Thérèse (DE 11 July, 7 July 1897).
- Note: From M-EW-022 (PO 1, f. 236v–238v, Session 18, 1 Sept 1910). Pauline insists these differed from ordinary recollection. Status in the source: sworn testimony; not an approved miracle. No medical or natural facts mentioned.
- Source entries: M-EW-022 (partial use: M-EW-022)

### M-101 — Two hosts, c. 1892
- Date: c. 1892
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: Troubled before Communion, she thought that if she received only half a host she would believe Jesus came reluctantly; "for the first time in my life" the priest gave her two separate hosts.
- The person helped: Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse (Ms A, 1895)
- Status in the sources: her own report of a consolation
- Source entries: M-OW-06
- Sources cited: Ms A 80r (F-OW-133); public-domain

### M-102 — Céline's dance partner unable to dance (c. 1889–94)
- Date: c. 1892
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; her own report; personal report or belief of the witness
- What was reported: After Thérèse wept and prayed that Céline would not dance at an evening party (a wedding party, c. 1892), Céline long avoided dancing, annoying several people (and put a large crucifix in her pocket). Near the end a young man led (literally swept) her onto the floor. She and her partner were "as though paralyzed": they could not do one dance step each time the music began, tried in vain to keep time, and walked with "a very religious step". He walked her back to her seat and slipped away "red with shame" for the rest of the evening. Céline went back laughing. She credits Thérèse's prayers.
- The person helped: [OW-07] Céline Martin [LF-009, EW-020, EW-021] Céline
- Witnesses and reporter: [OW-07] Céline (source of the story); Thérèse's account (Ms A, 1895) [LF-009] Céline (Summarium 1919 p.400 §1013) [EW-020] Céline [EW-021] Céline, involved
- Status in the sources: [OW-07] her own report of an answered prayer [LF-009] investigated by the Church (testimony) [EW-020] private memoir [EW-021] sworn testimony; not a miracle
- Where the sources differ: Date: c. 1890–94 (Laveille), c. 1889–93 (Céline's memoir), c. 1892 (Apostolic Process, Ms A). The wedding and the partner are unnamed. Céline says only "it was really strange" and calls herself "literally forced".
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [OW-07] Céline's own account (other parts) may name the occasion. [LF-009] The partner is unnamed. Céline calls herself "literally forced". [EW-020] Céline says only "it was really strange". [EW-021] Unnamed wedding and partner.
- Source entries: M-OW-07, M-LF-009, M-EW-020, M-EW-021
- Sources cited: [OW-07] Ms A 82r (F-OW-148); public-domain [LF-009] LAV p.249–250; F-LF-214 [EW-020] CS §V "Ne pas pactiser avec le siècle" [EW-021] PA 8, [750]–[752], Session 35

- Date: 18 Jul 1893
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: At prayer she asked Jesus to find a passage for Céline and "drew" the fig-tree verse, which she took as a sign that God's kingdom was set up in Céline's soul.

- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse
- Status in the sources: her own report of a devotional sign

### M-104 — Prayers to inspire Céline, "never refused"
- Date: 23 Jul 1893
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: Thérèse had asked Jesus to inspire Céline with the idea of "little sacrifices" before she wrote. Céline had the idea on her own. "Never yet has Our Lord refused me to inspire you with what I had asked Him to tell you."
- The person helped: Céline
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse, 23 July 1893
- Status in the sources: her own report of answered prayer

### M-105 — Aunt Sr Marie-Dosithée's prediction about Léonie
- Date: 13 Aug 1893
- Status label: family report or tradition; her own report
- What was reported: "So now our holy Aunt's prediction has come true": Léonie entered the Visitation.
- The person helped: Léonie
- Witnesses and reporter: The prediction was by "notre sainte Tante" (the Le Mans Visitandine). Thérèse reports it in 1893.
- Status in the sources: her own report of a family tradition
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): The letter does not quote the prediction. Léonie left the Visitation in July 1895 (LT 178). The file's text « L'enfant de la Breuse Marg. Marie » looks corrupt.

### M-106 — Céline's childhood dream of an angel with a torch
- Date: 26 Apr 1894
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: As a child Céline saw in a dream an angel carrying a torch and walking before their father. Thérèse read it as a sign of Céline's mission to be his "visible angel".
- The person helped: Céline; Louis Martin
- Witnesses and reporter: Céline dreamed it. Thérèse reports it second-hand.
- Status in the sources: her own report of her sister's dream
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Not in the 1911 text. Compare Ms A's own prophetic vision of her father (a different event).
- Source entries: M-OW-21
- Sources cited: LT 161 (to Céline, 26 Apr 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-161-a-celine-26-avril-1894/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-107 — A grace obtained at Lourdes for Céline Maudelonde
- Date: 16 Jul 1894
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: Thérèse had asked Our Lady of Mount Carmel for "the grace you obtained at Lourdes". She linked it to the cousin wearing the scapular and to her husband's shared faith.
- The person helped: Céline Maudelonde (Mme Pottier)
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse, 16 July 1894
- Status in the sources: her own report. The nature of the grace is not stated.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): LT 159 (she prayed that the cousin would bring her fiancé's soul to God)
- Source entries: M-OW-25
- Sources cited: LT 166, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-166-a-mme-pottier-celine-maudelonde-16-juillet-1894/ ; public-domain

### M-108 — The luminous globe at Louis's death, 29 July 1894
- Date: 29 Jul 1894
- Status label: popular report
- What was reported: Céline wrote to the Carmel that in the night before Louis died she saw a luminous globe in the sky. Louis died at 8.15 a.m. Isidore said he had never seen so peaceful a death.
- The person helped: none (a sign reported by Céline)
- Witnesses and reporter: Céline, 29 Jul 1894
- Status in the sources: popular tradition
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): A sign told by one witness in a grief letter.
- Source entries: M-LF-011
- Sources cited: Céline's letter, 29 Jul 1894 (raw-notes/family-letters.md); F-LF-215

### M-109 — The father's "miraculous catch" and help with Céline's vocation
- Date: 19 Aug 1894
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: Thérèse wrote that Céline's "pêche miraculeuse" ("miraculous catch") was one of the "little delicacies" showing their dead father was near. She said he, "now a pure spirit", had quickly made Céline's vocation succeed by going to "priests and bishops".
- The person helped: Céline Martin
- Witnesses and reporter: Céline (who experienced it); Thérèse wrote of it on 19 Aug 1894
- Status in the sources: her own report, as belief. Not investigated.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): The letter does not say what the "pêche miraculeuse" was. The 1911 text drops the phrase and adds "il n'y a pas un mois qu'il est au ciel, et déjà, par son intervention puissante, toutes tes démarches réussissent".
- Source entries: M-OW-19
- Sources cited: LT 169 (to Céline, 19 Aug 1894), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-169-a-celine-19-aout-1894/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-110 — Feeling her dead father's presence
- Date: 20 Aug 1894
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: "I find him again after 6 years of absence, I feel him around me looking at me and protecting me."
- The person helped: Thérèse (and her sisters)
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse, writing to Léonie, 20 Aug 1894
- Status in the sources: her own report of an inner experience
- Source entries: M-OW-20
- Sources cited: LT 170, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-170-a-soeur-therese-dosithee-leonie-20-aout-1894/ ; public-domain (her words)

### M-111 — The sign about her father: the opposing nun changes her mind about Céline's entry (summer 1894)
- Date: Sep 1894
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine; her own report
- What was reported: A nun (Sr Aimée de Jésus, "Sr A. de J.", identification by the modern editors) opposed the entry of a fourth Martin sister. Thérèse prayed to her dead father, after Communion, asking as a sign that he had gone straight to heaven that the obstacle be removed. Just after her thanksgiving that nun was the first person she met. As though almost ashamed of her former attitude, she called her kindly and told her earnestly, with tears, how happy she would be to see Céline in the Carmel.
- The person helped: [LF-007] Céline
- Witnesses and reporter: [OW-08] Thérèse (Ms A, 1895) [LF-007] Thérèse (as reported by Laveille); the nun is not named
- Status in the sources: [OW-08] her own report [LF-007] recorded by the monastery
- Where the sources differ: Date: 1894 (Ms A); Laveille says Aug–Sep 1894 with no independent witness. The nun is not named in Laveille.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [OW-08] The identification of "Sr A. de J." is the modern editors'. [LF-007] No date beyond Aug–Sep 1894; no independent witness
- Source entries: M-OW-08, M-LF-007
- Sources cited: [OW-08] Ms A 82v (F-OW-187); public-domain [LF-007] LAV p.251; F-LF-217

### M-112 — The wound of love after the Offering (June 1895)
- Date: Jun 1895
- Status label: recorded by a sister of the Carmel; her own report
- What was reported: A few days after her Offering to Merciful Love (9 Jun 1895), beginning the Way of the Cross (in choir), she felt "as if plunged entirely into fire" (a "dart of fire"); she thought she would die and one second more would have killed her. It happened "only once and only for an instant". She told Mère Agnès the same day (who "paid no attention"); it was recorded on 7 Jul 1897 in the Carnet jaune.
- The person helped: [OW-10] Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: [OW-10] told to Mère Agnès the same day (who "paid no attention"); recorded 7 Jul 1897 in the Carnet jaune
- Status in the sources: [OW-10] her own report, recorded by Mère Agnès
- Where the sources differ: Only in the last-words record, not in her manuscripts. Pauline admits she did not believe the wound of love in 1895 (F-EW-1054). Also told at the Ordinary Process (M-EW-022).
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [OW-10] Only in the last-words record, not in her manuscripts.
- Source entries: M-OW-10, M-EW-022 (partial use: M-EW-022)
- Sources cited: [OW-10] CJ 7.7.2 (F-OW-252); public-domain; transcription unknown

### M-113 — The novice who was "instantaneously and extraordinarily changed"
- Date: c. 1895
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine
- What was reported: A religious, before her profession, worn out and in interior trial, asked Thérèse for comfort before evening prayer. Thérèse said, "There is the bell for Prayer; I have no time now to console you… God wills you to suffer alone for the moment." In chapel the novice doubted her vocation for the first time, then felt "an instantaneous and extraordinary change" and came out "absolutely transformed". Next day Thérèse said she had felt such pity for her that she had prayed for her at the start of Prayer.
- The person helped: an unnamed novice
- Witnesses and reporter: the novice, quoted in *Conseils et souvenirs* (LAV p.293–294, citing p.291)
- Status in the sources: recorded by the monastery
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Name and date not given.
- Source entries: M-LF-008
- Sources cited: LAV p.293–294; rights: public-domain

### M-114 — "You are unhappy": a novice's hidden sorrow
- Date: c. 1895
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: She told a novice who hid a sorrow "You are unhappy"; the novice was stunned; Thérèse herself felt "a supernatural fright", being sure she had no gift of reading souls. Novices believed she read their souls.
- The person helped: an unnamed novice
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse (Ms C, 1897)
- Status in the sources: her own report; she denies any special gift
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Novices' testimony in other parts.
- Source entries: M-OW-09
- Sources cited: Ms C 26r (F-OW-142); public-domain

### M-115 — Dream of Venerable Anne of Jesus, 10 May 1896
- Date: 10 May 1896
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: In a dream at dawn, three Carmelites "from Heaven" came; Venerable Anne of Jesus covered her with her veil and told her God would come for her "soon, soon" and that he was "very pleased" with her. Thérèse said it made her believe and feel "that there is a Heaven", in the middle of her trial of faith.
- The person helped: Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse (Ms B, Sept 1896)
- Status in the sources: her own report of a dream
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): She died 16 months later.
- Source entries: M-OW-11
- Sources cited: Ms B 2r–2v (F-OW-214); public-domain

### M-116 — Sr Thérèse de Saint-Augustin's dream of the black door, 8 Jan 1897
- Date: 8 Jan 1897
- Status label: recorded by a sister of the Carmel
- What was reported: A fellow nun dreamed a voice called for Sr Thérèse of the Child Jesus behind a black door and said "She must be very beautiful!" Thérèse said it was "a vision... for me" and "my only ray of light".
- The person helped: Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: Sr Thérèse de Saint-Augustin's written account; summarised by Mère Agnès in her obituary circular
- Status in the sources: recorded by a sister; not investigated
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Later account; wording may be reworked.
- Source entries: M-OW-12
- Sources cited: Dernières paroles à d'autres soeurs (F-OW-220); public-domain; transcription unknown

### M-117 — A drink after a silent complaint, spring 1897
- Date: Apr 1897
- Status label: recorded by a sister of the Carmel
- What was reported: Burning with fever and thirst, given a hot-water bottle and iodine on her chest, she told Jesus she would like half a glass of water instead; the infirmarian, who was not expected back until morning, returned minutes later with a cool drink.
- The person helped: Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse told Sr Marie de la Trinité, who recorded it
- Status in the sources: recorded by a novice
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): fever, thirst; hot-water bottle; tincture of iodine on the chest
- Source entries: M-OW-13
- Sources cited: Dernières paroles à d'autres soeurs, "Marie de la Trinité" n. 6 (see F-OW-288 context); public-domain; transcription unknown

### M-118 — The novena of Masses at Our Lady of Victories (June–July 1897), and her sense that she would not be cured
- Date: 9 Jun 1897
- Status label: her own report
- Flag: no extraordinary claim, or no outcome: listed only because the source reports it
- What was reported: In June 1897 the prioress had a novena of Masses said at Notre-Dame des Victoires for her cure. Thérèse writes that Our Lady of Victories "had already cured me in my childhood", but that she felt "the miracle would not take place" and asked instead that the Virgin console the prioress and make her consent to her death.
- The person helped: Thérèse (intended); the prioress (in her view)
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse, in two letters to Bellière
- Status in the sources: her own report; the novena was not answered by a cure
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): frequent haemoptysis from 6 July (editors' note); the doctor's shifting views (CJ 30–31 July).
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): the childhood cure is the Virgin's smile of May 1883 (Ms A, baseline); the letters call the shrine "Notre-Dame des Victoires", not the statue of the smile.
- Source entries: M-OW-36
- Sources cited: LT 244 (9 Jun 1897) and LT 253 (13 Jul 1897), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-244-a-labbe-belliere-9-juin-1897/ ; https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-253-a-labbe-belliere-13-juillet-1897/ ; public-domain

### M-119 — "The Thief is in my heart" and the "little thief" (25–31 July 1897)
- Date: 25 Jul 1897
- Status label: recorded by a sister of the Carmel
- What was reported: She calls death "the Thief" who will come like a thief in the night. On 25 July she answers "Where is the Thief now?" with "He is here! He is in my heart." On 31 July she says she will be "a little thief" in Heaven and "take everything that pleases me", and after a large spitting of blood in the night she looked at the Virgin's statue and said she had thought "you were going to take me away".
- The person helped: Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: Mère Agnès, recording her words, July 1897 (notebook copied 1921–23)
- Status in the sources: recorded by her sister; not investigated
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): large haemoptysis in the night 30–31 July.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): the "Thief" image is from the Gospel (God comes like a thief); Novissima Verba has "thief" sayings.
- Source entries: M-OW-37
- Sources cited: CJ 25.7.5; CJ 31.7.5–6, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/ ; public-domain (1897 words); transcription unknown

### M-120 — Dream of being carried to the warming room between two torches (27 July 1897)
- Date: 27 Jul 1897
- Status label: recorded by a sister of the Carmel
- Flag: no extraordinary claim, or no outcome: listed only because the source reports it
- What was reported: She told Mère Agnès with a laugh that she had dreamed that she was carried to the warming room (chauffoir) between two torches for "Our Father's" feast.
- The person helped: Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: Mère Agnès, 27 Jul 1897
- Status in the sources: recorded by her sister (a dream told as a joke); not investigated
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): she also told a dream on 26 July of a bazaar with her father (CJ 26.7.1). Neither is presented by her as a sign.
- Source entries: M-OW-38
- Sources cited: CJ 27.7.3, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/ ; public-domain (1897 words)

### M-121 — Her father's "guarantee" of 29 July 1897 and the "passport for Heaven"
- Date: 29 Jul 1897
- Status label: her own report
- What was reported: In a letter of 10 Aug 1897 she writes that she has "received my passport for Heaven", that her dear father obtained this grace, and that "on the 29th he gave me the guarantee that I would soon join him". The letter does not say what the guarantee was (a dream, a feeling, or a sign).
- The person helped: Thérèse
- Witnesses and reporter: Thérèse herself, writing to Abbé Bellière
- Status in the sources: her own report; not investigated
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): the doctor found the illness advanced "in two days" (30 July) and advised extreme unction.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): 29 July is the anniversary of her father's death (29 Jul 1894, other cards). The Carnet jaune for 29 July records no such event.
- Source entries: M-OW-35
- Sources cited: LT 263 (to l'abbé Bellière, 10 Aug 1897), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-263-a-labbe-belliere-10-aout-1897/ ; public-domain

### M-122 — Pain moved to her left side "for Céline", 16 Aug 1897
- Date: 16 Aug 1897
- Status label: recorded by a sister of the Carmel
- What was reported: She felt the devil tormenting her; God asked if she would suffer for Céline; at once her left side was seized with great pain; Céline lit a blessed candle and calm returned.
- The person helped: Céline (spiritually)
- Witnesses and reporter: Sr Geneviève (Céline)
- Status in the sources: recorded by her sister
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): the Carmel editors' note says the left lung was being affected in mid-August (tuberculosis)
- Source entries: M-OW-14
- Sources cited: Dernières paroles à Céline, 16 Aug (F-OW-289); public-domain

### M-123 — Reading Marie's heart (Carmel infirmary, September 1897)
- Date: Sep 1897
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: (1) Marie felt a secret despair at losing her, which had faded when she came in without any sign of grief; Thérèse at once said "we must not weep like those who have no hope". (2) Eight days before the death Marie told Agnès she wished Thérèse loved her, who had also raised her, as she loved Agnès. That afternoon, alone with both, Thérèse looked at them and said: "My little sisters, it is you who raised me."
- The person helped: Marie
- Witnesses and reporter: Marie, involved; Agnès present at (2)
- Status in the sources: sworn testimony; not a miracle
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Céline stresses Thérèse herself denied reading souls (MS C: "I don't have the gift of reading souls"), [776].
- Source entries: M-EW-029
- Sources cited: PA 7, [603]–[604], Session 25

### M-124 — Petitions granted for Fr Granger, September 1897
- Date: Sep 1897
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine
- What was reported: Fr Granger, a missionary of La Délivrande and friend of Canon Faucon, asked her prayers for "two very great favours". She promised, and the petitions were "soon fully granted". The favours are not named.
- The person helped: Fr Granger
- Witnesses and reporter: Laveille (no note)
- Status in the sources: recorded by the monastery
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Very thin; recorded only as a passing remark.
- Source entries: M-LF-010
- Sources cited: LAV p.394

### M-125 — Message about "Father Corbinelli", Sept 1897
- Date: Sep 1897
- Status label: recorded by a sister of the Carmel
- What was reported: The old Mère Cœur de Jésus, who wished to die soon and be "fetched" by Thérèse, received in Thérèse's last days the message: "this morning during Mass I saw the tomb of Father Corbinelli very close to that of little Louis." The recorder calls it "truly prophetic".
- Witnesses and reporter: Sr Hermance du Cœur de Jésus's account
- Status in the sources: recorded by a sister
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): The account does not give Mère Cœur de Jésus's death date; check.
- Source entries: M-OW-16
- Sources cited: Dernières paroles à d'autres soeurs (F-OW-326)

### M-126 — Answer to an unspoken thought: the spittoon, 21 Sep 1897
- Date: 21 Sep 1897
- Status label: recorded by a sister of the Carmel
- What was reported: Mère Agnès silently emptied her spittoon wishing Thérèse would say she would repay it in Heaven; Thérèse at once said "In Heaven I'll repay you for that."
- Witnesses and reporter: Mère Agnès
- Status in the sources: recorded by Mère Agnès
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): coincidence possible; recorded in a copy of 1921–1923
- Source entries: M-OW-15
- Sources cited: CJ 21.9.1 (F-OW-320)

### M-127 — The dead leaf that fell when she died (30 September 1897)
- Date: 30 Sep 1897
- Status label: recorded by a sister of the Carmel
- What was reported: On 26 Sep she pointed to a dead leaf hanging on a spider's thread: "my life hangs by no more than a thread". Mère Agnès reports that the leaf fell on the evening of her death and that she kept it with its thread.
- Witnesses and reporter: Mère Agnès
- Status in the sources: recorded by Mère Agnès
- Source entries: M-OW-17
- Sources cited: CJ 26.9 (F-OW-327)

### M-128 — The "ecstasy" at the moment of death (Carmel infirmary, 30 September 1897, about 7.15–7.20 p.m.)
- Date: 30 Sep 1897
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; recorded by her sisters; described as an "ecstasy of love", not a miracle
- What was reported: After her last words ("My God, I love you") she raised her head, which had been bent, and fixed a point just above the statue of Mary for several minutes (about the space of a Creed). Her purple, congested face took on its healthy lily colour, became white, almost transparent and very beautiful, and her eyes shone with peace and joy. Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie held a candle close and the eyelids did not move. Marie: the look recalled the 1883 cure. Céline: she sat up "as if called by a heavenly voice", with astonishment, as if unable to bear so much love; Céline thinks they witnessed her judgment. Afterwards, according to Mère Agnès, a "heavenly smile"; she looked 12 or 13.
- Witnesses and reporter: [OW-18] the community; Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie (candle); Mère Agnès (recorder); Sr Aimée de Jésus and Marie (laying out) [EW-030] Mère Agnès and the whole community recalled for the moment [EW-031] Marie, Céline, Mother Agnès, Mother Marie de Gonzague and the community (called back)
- Status in the sources: [OW-18] recorded by her sisters; later used in the process [EW-030] sworn testimony; not an approved miracle [EW-031] sworn testimony; described as "ecstasy of love"; not a miracle
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): [OW-18] before death: rattle for over two hours, congested face, purple hands, icy feet, trembling, heavy sweating [EW-030] long death rattle, cyanosis, sweat before [EW-031] dried tongue; congested face; cold sweat; gasping breath; trembling
- Where the sources differ: Time: 7.15 (Marie) or 7.20 (Céline). Pauline's oral version says she then closed her eyes and sighed several times; the NV notebook says she "gave her last breath" right after. Who saw it: the community was called back (Marie, Céline, Mère Agnès, Mother Marie de Gonzague). Sr Aimée de Jésus and Marie laid her out.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [EW-030] The oral version says she then closed her eyes and sighed several times; the NV notebook says she "gave her last breath" right after. [EW-031] Time 7:15 (Marie) vs 7:20 (Céline).
- Source entries: M-OW-18, M-EW-030, M-EW-031
- Sources cited: [OW-18] CJ 30.9 (F-OW-334) [EW-030] PO 1, f. 246v, Session 19; NV f. 292r–v [EW-031] PA 7, [617], Session 26; PA 8, [794]–[795], Session 38

### M-129 — The oratory mural painted without training
- Date: undated
- Status label: personal report or belief of the witness
- What was reported: Céline calls "absolutely supernatural" that Thérèse, never taught, painted an original mural of little angels in the dark oratory.
- Witnesses and reporter: Céline, saw the work
- Status in the sources: Céline's personal judgment
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Natural explanation (talent) is obvious; Céline, a painter, insists on it.
- Source entries: M-EW-105
- Sources cited: PA 8, [777], Session 37

## D. After her death (sworn testimony, Carmel records and popular reports)

### M-130 — Céline's three inner "signs" from Thérèse (October 1897; a voice; June 1908)
- Date: Oct 1897
- Status label: sworn at the Church process (Céline alone); not an approved miracle
- What was reported: In Oct 1897 Céline saw "a flame" while making the Way of the Cross in the cloister and cried "It is Thérèse". Later she heard the words "Bien humblement!" and, in June 1908 at 4 a.m., "One gives oneself to Love only as far as one gives oneself to suffering."
- The person helped: Céline (Sr Geneviève)
- Witnesses and reporter: Céline herself, in her own deposition; no other witness
- Status in the sources: sworn testimony at the Ordinary Process; not an approved miracle
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): See F-EW-2483 to F-EW-2485.
- Note: See also M-135 (the ring of light around the moon, mid-October 1897), which may be the same event as the first sign.
- Source entries: M-EW-190
- Sources cited: PO 4, f. 409v–410v, S34, W1; rights: facts-only

### M-131 — A lay sister cured of "cerebral anaemia" by kissing her feet (Carmel, October 1897)
- Date: Oct 1897
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; recorded by the Carmel or shrine
- What was reported: A lay sister of the Lisieux Carmel who had once spoken unkindly to Thérèse (over the arrangement of wreaths round Mère Geneviève's coffin, F-LF-207) had suffered for many years from "cerebral anaemia", which kept her from reading or mental prayer. Repentant, she kissed the dead Thérèse's feet, leaned her forehead on them, asked pardon, and "felt herself suddenly cured". Céline (1915) heard from the sister herself that she owed Thérèse the cure.
- The person helped: [LF-012] an unnamed lay sister [OM-005] a lay sister of the Lisieux Carmel (not named in these sources) [EW-032] unnamed lay sister of the Lisieux Carmel (tradition: Sr Saint-Vincent de Paul — to verify in batch I/K) [EW-107] Sr Saint-Vincent de Paul, lay sister (dead by 1915)
- Witnesses and reporter: [LF-012] Laveille [OM-005] the Carmel's account (Taylor); Laveille [EW-032] the Prioress's Epilogue (D6) [EW-107] Céline, hearsay from the sister
- Status in the sources: [LF-012] recorded by the monastery [OM-005, EW-032] recorded by the Carmel [EW-107] sworn testimony (hearsay); not an approved miracle
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): [LF-012] "cerebral anaemia" (as Laveille writes) [OM-005] none beyond the name of the illness. [EW-032] "cerebral anaemia" of many years [EW-107] "anémie cérébrale"
- Where the sources differ: The sister is unnamed in Laveille and Taylor. M-EW-032 gives the tradition that she was Sr Saint-Vincent de Paul ("to verify"); M-EW-107 names her and says she was dead by 1915. No doctor is named and there is no date beyond 30 Sep – 4 Oct 1897.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [LF-012] Name and doctor not given. [EW-032] name not given [EW-107] No date or medical detail.
- Source entries: M-LF-012, M-OM-005, M-EW-032, M-EW-107
- Sources cited: [LF-012] LAV p.399 [OM-005] Laveille PDF 399; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 267 [EW-032] D6 p. 197 (PDF 231) [EW-107] PA 8, [805], Session 39

### M-132 — Signs around her death: perfumes, a light, a crown, a kiss (Carmel, October 1897)
- Date: Oct 1897
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine
- What was reported: One nun smelled violets; one felt a kiss from an invisible being; one saw a light in the sky; another saw a luminous crown rise from the earth and vanish. Laveille: several nuns noticed a perfume of lilies and violets near Thérèse's body where no flowers grew. A child of ten among the visitors at the grille smelled lilies, though only artificial lilies were there.
- Status in the sources: [OM-006] recorded by the Carmel
- Note: M-LF-013 (part 1) reports the perfume of lilies and violets from Laveille p. 399. See also M-133 (a separate report of 1 Oct 1897) and the general reports in M-312.
- Source entries: M-OM-006, M-LF-013 (partial use: M-LF-013)
- Sources cited: [OM-006] Taylor 1926/30 PDF 267; Laveille PDF 399

### M-133 — Violet perfume in Sr Marie de Saint-Joseph's cell (1 Oct 1897)
- Date: 1 Oct 1897
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: On the evening of Friday 1 Oct 1897, the day after the death, Sr Marie de Saint-Joseph found her cell filled with a scent of violets so strong she thought someone had left a bouquet and searched everywhere for it.
- The person helped: Sr Marie de Saint-Joseph, a sister to whom Thérèse had shown great charity
- Witnesses and reporter: reported by Marie (not present)
- Status in the sources: sworn testimony, hearsay
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Céline says perfumes began for her only in 1908 and "had not occurred in the 11 years after the death" — at least for her (C-M09).
- Source entries: M-EW-034
- Sources cited: PO 3, f. 330v, S26

### M-134 — Supple limbs and a smile after death (30 September – 4 October 1897)
- Date: 1 Oct 1897
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: After her death her limbs stayed supple until burial on 4 Oct 1897; she kept a gentle smile ("a heavenly smile"), looked 12 or 13 and had great beauty (Mère Agnès; Sr Aimée de Jésus and Marie laid her out).
- Witnesses and reporter: [EW-035] Mère Agnès
- Status in the sources: [EW-035] sworn testimony; not an approved miracle
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): [EW-035] Pauline also says the coffin was closed on 3 Oct because "some signs of decomposition" appeared, and that nothing extraordinary happened at the burial (f. 246v–247r).
- Where the sources differ: Pauline presents the supple limbs without claiming a miracle. She also says the coffin was closed on 3 Oct because "some signs of decomposition" appeared, and that nothing extraordinary happened at the burial (f. 246v–247r).
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [EW-035] Pauline presents the supple limbs without claiming a miracle.
- Note: M-OW-18 (CJ 30.9, F-OW-334) is used for the smile and the supple body; its account of the "ecstasy" is in M-128.
- Source entries: M-EW-035, M-OW-18 (partial use: M-OW-18)
- Sources cited: [EW-035] PO 1, NV f. 292v

### M-135 — A ring of light around the moon (Céline, mid-October 1897)
- Date: 15 Oct 1897
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; personal report or belief of the witness
- What was reported: Fifteen days after the death Céline saw in the sky a luminous circle traced by a flame that seemed alive; it left the right side of the full moon, circled beneath it, and re-entered on the left. It gave her a strong inner grace and sudden understanding of many things Thérèse had told her. She was sure it was her sister's soul.
- The person helped: Céline
- Witnesses and reporter: Céline alone
- Status in the sources: sworn testimony; personal
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): full moon (a halo is a natural possibility; not discussed)
- Note: Possibly the same event as the "flame" in Céline's deposition in M-130 (PO 4 places a flame in the cloister during the Way of the Cross in Oct 1897; this deposition, PA 8, describes a flame-traced circle at the full moon fifteen days after the death). Kept apart because the settings differ.
- Source entries: M-EW-033
- Sources cited: PA 8, [806], Session 39

### M-136 — Céline's Gospel lot after Thérèse's death (Lisieux Carmel, late 1897)
- Date: Nov 1897
- Status label: personal report or belief of the witness
- What was reported: A sister offered to help Céline obtain an object of Thérèse's. Céline asked Thérèse and opened the Gospel at random: "like a man going on a journey who leaves his house and gives power to his servants" (Mt 25:14). She took it as Thérèse's answer.
- The person helped: Céline
- Witnesses and reporter: Céline
- Status in the sources: personal
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Céline does not say what she then did.
- Source entries: M-EW-028
- Sources cited: CS §V "Pauvreté"

### M-137 — The broken sandal (Marie de l'Eucharistie, winter 1897–98)
- Date: Jan 1898
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; family report or tradition
- What was reported: Marie de l'Eucharistie, with frozen feet, chose to mortify herself and not put on her warm alpargates (rope-soled sandals), which she had permission to wear because of nosebleeds. As she passed the room where they dried, one of her cold sandals broke beyond repair, so she had to put on the warm ones. She saw it as one of "my little Thérèse's tricks".
- The person helped: Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie (Marie Guérin)
- Witnesses and reporter: her own letter to her father, 3 July 1898, filed by Jeanne
- Status in the sources: family letter in sworn testimony at the Ordinary Process; a "little grace", not a miracle claim
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): frequent nosebleeds; rope soles soak up damp; foot-warmers forbidden for warming feet in Carmel
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): a natural event read as a sign The letter adds that Thérèse, as novice guide, was "always full of tenderness" and often stopped her novices from suffering, then let them sacrifice and found ways to lessen it.
- Source entries: M-EW-025
- Sources cited: PO-20 [1154v–1155r]

### M-138 — Fruit from Brest, as promised for the blackbirds
- Date: 1898
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: The year after her death a case of pears and a basket of strawberries arrived, sent by the superior of the Hospital of Brest, whom the Carmel did not know, "to please Sr Thérèse". The gift came again every year.
- The person helped: the Carmel (Marie, provisoire)
- Witnesses and reporter: Marie
- Status in the sources: sworn testimony; presented as a fulfilled promise
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): The Brest superior's motive is given as her own words; natural explanation possible (the first edition had appeared in 1898).
- Source entries: M-EW-103
- Sources cited: PO 3, f. 323v–324r, S25

- What was reported: Dissolute colonial infantryman read her Life in hospital, reformed, gave up smoking (May 1900), wrote 19 Jun 1900 of his "daring confidence"; died of sunstroke before reaching Tientsin. Reporter: prioress of the Carmel of Saigon, 31 May 1910. Taylor 1924 PDF 423–425. (Taylor prints "China" for Saigon; the "May, 1900" dates are as printed.)

### M-140 — Mother Marie de Gonzague's "signal grace" before a portrait of the child Thérèse (1897–1904)
- Date: c. 1900
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: Before a portrait of Thérèse as a child on her mother's knee, Mother Marie de Gonzague (prioress, died 1904) received "a signal grace, known to herself alone", and could never look at the portrait afterwards without tears. She said to Céline: "I alone can know what I owe her. Oh, what she said to me! ... but so tenderly" (only she knew what Thérèse had reproached her with).
- The person helped: [LF-016] Marie de Gonzague [EW-108] Mother Marie de Gonzague (d. 1904)
- Witnesses and reporter: [LF-016] Céline (Summarium 1919 p.994 §2854) [EW-108] Céline, saw the tears
- Status in the sources: [LF-016] investigated by the Church (testimony) [EW-108] sworn testimony; spiritual favour
- Where the sources differ: The nature of the grace is not stated in either source. Céline testified as eyewitness of the tears (Apostolic Process PA 8 [805]; Summarium 1919 p. 994 §2854).
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [LF-016] The grace is not described. [EW-108] Nature of the grace not stated.
- Source entries: M-LF-016, M-EW-108
- Sources cited: [LF-016] LAV p.401–402; F-LF-243 [EW-108] PA 8, [805], Session 39

### M-143 — Pierre-Marie Derrien, Breton postal courier and later sacristan of the Carmel: illness, inner voice and cure
- Date: 1904
- Status label: popular report
- What was reported: Pierre Derrien (born 1880), a Breton postal courier, had a grave illness and "had been abandoned by the doctors". Dolan (from Pierre himself and local knowledge, 1926) says he invoked Thérèse in 1904 and was "immediately cured"; he went to Lisieux to thank her, then alone to Rome, where Pius X received him privately, and Mère Agnès made him guardian of the grave. Derrien's own sworn account (Apostolic Process, 29–30 Sept 1916, as co-witness ex officio) is more detailed: three years of acute bronchitis, typhoid and muco-membranous enteritis; an inner voice said "Pierre, I am looking for souls for Jesus"; on 21 March 1911 the voice said a full cure would make him offend God again; he accepted suffering and recovered gradually without remedies. At the tomb in March 1913 the voice said "ask to come into my service"; the Carmel at first had no need. After blood-spitting on a Rome pilgrimage (June 1913) he recovered after a novena. On 29 Sept 1913 at 4 p.m. a telegram from the prioress asked if he would become "little Thérèse's servant". He became sacristan of the Carmel chapel. He also found coins on the road when he wanted to help the poor.
- The person helped: [EW-010] Pierre-Marie Derrien [EW-039] Pierre Derrien, man from Brittany
- Witnesses and reporter: [EW-010] himself [EW-039] Dolan, from Pierre and local knowledge, 1926 [OM-014] Pierre himself, interviewed by Dolan (1926)
- Status in the sources: [EW-010] co-witness ex officio at the Apostolic Process, 29–30 Sept 1916, pp. 1313–1325; not approved miracles [EW-039] popular report (not examined in the source) [OM-014] popular report
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): [EW-010] bronchitis, typhoid, enteritis; haemoptysis; he stopped all medicines [EW-039] illness unnamed; "incurable" [OM-014] illness not named; doctors had given up.
- Where the sources differ: Date and manner: Dolan (M-EW-039, M-OM-014) says 1904 and immediate; Derrien himself (M-EW-010) places the gradual recovery in 1911, without remedies. He was the Carmel's own employee when testifying. Dolan reports the private audience with Pius X; Derrien's testimony as summarized here does not mention it.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [EW-010] he was the Carmel's own employee when testifying [EW-039] no medical detail
- Source entries: M-EW-010, M-EW-039, M-OM-014
- Sources cited: [EW-010] PA-25 file, co-witness 2, [1314–1324] [EW-039] D4 ch. X, pp. 109–110 (PDF 139–140); p. 42 (PDF 56) [OM-014] Dolan PDF 137–138

### M-144 — The bandits' powder blows up (Sichuan, c. 1904)
- Date: c. 1904
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: During a persecution about 200 Christian women and girls took refuge at Roulland's residence. In his absence, bandits prepared to attack. Before setting out they set off firecrackers to their gods; one lit their powder, blew up the pagoda, killed or burned many bandits, and the rest fled. The women were safe before they knew the danger.
- The person helped: Roulland's Christian women and his mission
- Witnesses and reporter: Roulland (absent at the time)
- Status in the sources: sworn testimony at the Ordinary Process; a providential protection, not a miracle claim
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): an accident read as protection; Roulland was not present
- Source entries: M-EW-040
- Sources cited: PO-09 [535v–536r]

- What was reported: Céline was convinced Thérèse inspired her plan to copy the Holy Face from the Turin Shroud and caused its success.

- Witnesses and reporter: Céline
- Status in the sources: personal belief
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): none given.

### M-147 — Sr Jeanne-Marie de l'Enfant-Jésus (Jeanne-Marie Halgand): graces, perfume and voices (1905–1912)
- Date: c. 1907
- Status label: sworn at the Church process (co-witness ex officio, Apostolic Process 29 Sept 1916, pp. 1307–1313); not approved miracles
- What was reported: A lay sister of the Lisieux Carmel (born 1886 at Crossac, a fisherman's daughter; entered 11 Nov 1905) testified that a look at Thérèse's image gave her strength in scruples and temptations against faith and certainty she would enter Carmel. The temptations ended on her clothing day (15 Oct 1906). In Oct 1907, alone in the kitchen for about two weeks, she did three people's work without fatigue and every morning found her cell smelling of incense. In 1909 an inner voice spoke to her about prayer. On 10 May 1912 Thérèse's voice woke her urging a sacrifice. Her kettle and box of relic slips are separate entries: M-163 and M-178.
- Note: From M-EW-014 (PA-25 file, co-witness 1, [1308–1312]). Witnesses: herself; Sr Marie-Madeleine (kettle); the prioress (box). Medical or natural facts: scruples, temptations against faith. She went to check with the prioress for fear of illusion; the kettle had been emptied and cleaned just before. She also says she smelled violets three times.
- Source entries: M-EW-014 (partial use: M-EW-014)

### M-148 — The Eure family (1): a woman cured of tuberculosis (c. 1907)
- Date: c. 1907

- What was reported: The letter-writer says Thérèse cured her of "tubercular disease" two years before her letter of March 1909 (about 1907).
