### M-304 — Léonie's sudden change after the aunt's death (Alençon, 1877)
- Date: Feb 1877
- Status label: family report or tradition
- What was reported: Just after Sr Marie-Dosithée died (24 Feb 1877), Léonie suddenly stopped clinging to Louise and threw herself into her mother's arms. Marie saw "a supernatural intervention" by the aunt.
- The person helped: Léonie Martin
- Witnesses and reporter: Marie
- Status in the sources: family memory
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Other family letters attribute the change to Marie's discovery of Louise's harshness; Marie says she herself had "sworn" to free Léonie.
- Source entries: M-EW-006
- Sources cited: MSC, Cahier

### M-305 — Zélie at Lourdes, June 1877: no cure
- Date: Jun 1877
- Status label: family report or tradition
- Flag: no extraordinary claim, or no outcome: listed only because the source reports it
- What was reported: Zélie went with Léonie, Marie and Pauline to Lourdes, hoping at least for Léonie's cure. She went into the icy pool four times and nearly choked. She was not cured, and Léonie was not cured. She wrote that God could work a miracle for her "even if nobody in the world knew of it".
- The person helped: none reported
- Witnesses and reporter: Zélie (CF 204–210)
- Status in the sources: recorded by the family (no cure)
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): Zélie's tumour; a doctor "who believes in nothing" (F-LF-095)
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): A young woman from Alençon died two days after arriving at Lourdes (CF 206).
- Source entries: M-LF-044
- Sources cited: CF 205, CF 209; F-LF-096, F-LF-097; rights: public-domain letters

### M-306 — Pietro Schilirò, Monza, 2002 (beatification miracle for Louis and Zélie)
- Date: 29 Jun 2002
- Status label: approved for the beatification of Louis and Zélie Martin (miracle approved 3 Jul 2008; beatification 19 Oct 2008); not attributed to Thérèse
- What was reported: Pietro, born 25 May 2002 in Monza with a severe lung malformation and unable to breathe unaided, was the subject of a novena to Louis and Zélie suggested by a Carmelite. His condition turned on 29 Jun 2002. He left the clinic by 27 Jul 2002.
- The person helped: Pietro Schilirò, newborn; parents Valter (Walter) and Adele
- Witnesses and reporter: the parents; the clinic; the diocesan inquiry closed at Milan 10 Jun 2003 (Card. Tettamanzi)
- Status in the sources: approved for beatification (miracle approved 3 Jul 2008; beatification 19 Oct 2008)
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): severe lung malformation; respiratory support (as reported)
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): The clinic-discharge date has medium confidence; Fr Sangalli's first name was not verified.
- Source entries: M-LF-042
- Sources cited: https://louisandzeliemartin.org/history-of-the-cause ; https://www.louisandzeliemartin.org/the-beatification-miracle ; https://www.ilgiornale.it/news/tettamanzi-celebra-guarigione-miracolosa-monza.html (via helper); facts-only

### M-307 — Carmen Pérez Pons, Valencia, 2008 (canonization miracle)
- Date: 19 Oct 2008
- Status label: approved for the canonization of Louis and Zélie Martin (miracle approved 18 Mar 2015; canonization 18 Oct 2015); not attributed to Thérèse
- What was reported: Carmen was born on 15 Oct 2008 in Valencia at about six months' gestation, with a grade 4 intraventricular brain haemorrhage plus infection and lung and heart problems. The Carmelites of Serra gave the family prayer cards of Louis and Zélie. Prayer began around 19 Oct 2008. She improved within days, left intensive care on the third day, and went home on 2 Jan 2009.
- The person helped: Carmen Pérez Pons, newborn; parents Carmen and Santos
- Witnesses and reporter: parents; eight doctors testified at the diocesan inquiry (7 Jan – 21 May 2013)
- Status in the sources: approved for canonization (miracle approved 18 Mar 2015; canonization 18 Oct 2015)
- Medical or natural facts (as in the sources): grade 4 intraventricular haemorrhage, infection, lung and heart problems (as reported)
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): ACI Prensa wrongly says she was born four days after the beatification (it was four days before). Inquiry close: 21 May vs 27 May 2013.
- Source entries: M-LF-043
- Sources cited: https://therecord.com.au/news/international/extraordinary-story-of-carmen-leads-to-canonisation-for-parents-of-st-therese/ ; https://www.louisandzeliemartin.org/canonization-miracle ; https://www.aciprensa.com/noticias/54764/... (via helper); facts-only

### M-308 — Mother Geneviève and the butter, the apricots, the thaw
- Date: undated
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine
- What was reported: (1) The cook said the butter was finished; Mother Geneviève said to use what was left. After two months Sister Madeleine said the small piece never grew less; a few days later an alms came and the pot was empty. (2) A hard frost withered the apricot blossom; the foundress prayed and heard "Be not disturbed, there will be apricots"; the tree bore so much fruit that leaves were hardly visible. (3) On a bitter washing day, while she prayed before the tabernacle, a thaw set in "like spring".
- The person helped: the community
- Witnesses and reporter: Sister Madeleine and others, in FND
- Status in the sources: recorded by the monastery
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): FND also says hers was "not a life of continual marvels".
- Source entries: M-LF-019
- Sources cited: FND p.42–43

## F. Appendix 2: collective and summary reports, and predictions (not single events)

### M-309 — Her promises of a mission after death: "a shower of roses" (1897)
- Date: 9 Jun 1897
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: In her last months Thérèse promised to "make roses rain" after death, right after a refectory reading on St Aloysius (Marie); Céline says she promised it "many times and in varied forms". She said her relics would be sought (keep her nail clippings); petals she touched would "make pleasures" later; she would spend her heaven doing good on earth; "it will be like a shower of roses" (9 June, to Marie); the manuscript must be published quickly or the devil would set traps; she would "come back"; she would "go round little baptized children" like a white lady in a mission report (25 June); "Ma sœur *** aura besoin de moi... je reviendrai". Related sayings in the own-writings part (no entry of its own there): "I will come down" (F-OW-309); "I will begin my mission... help the missionaries" (F-OW-261); "I will come and fetch you" (to Céline, F-OW-258); rose petals to be kept "to give pleasure later" (F-OW-317).
- Witnesses and reporter: [EW-027] Marie (HEARD); Céline [EW-102] Mère Agnès; her sisters present
- Status in the sources: [EW-027] prophetic saying; widely cited [EW-102] sworn testimony; not an approved miracle
- Where the sources differ: Marie gives a specific trigger (the St Aloysius reading); other accounts give other settings. Pauline recorded the sayings and later organized the Cause; the NV notebook was handed in only in 1910. The favours reported after 1897 are in the other entries.
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [EW-027] Marie gives a specific trigger; other accounts give other settings. [EW-102] Recorded by Pauline, who later organized the Cause; the NV notebook was handed in only in 1910.
- Source entries: M-EW-027, M-EW-102
- Sources cited: [EW-027] MSC Feuilles ajoutées; CS §IV "Après sa mort" [EW-102] PO 1, f. 238v–239r, Session 18; NV f. 253r, 254v, 258r, 263v, 270r, 287r

### M-310 — Prediction of daily Communion, judged fulfilled after her death (1897)
- Date: Oct 1897
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine; personal report or belief of the witness
- What was reported: Thérèse told Marie: "It will not be always so. A time will come when we shall have perhaps as chaplain M. l'Abbé Hodierne, and he will allow us Holy Communion every day." She predicted that after her death the nuns would not lack their "daily bread", and (Marie, Céline) that from heaven she would make Mother Marie de Gonzague change her mind. Chaplain Youf died a few days after her (Oct 1897); his successor Abbé Hodierne was named, and his first instruction took the text "Come and eat my Bread", inviting daily Communion before the community asked. Gonzague's "prejudices fell at once".
- The person helped: [EW-026, LF-006] the community
- Witnesses and reporter: [EW-026] Céline; editor's note [LF-006] Marie (Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur); Laveille
- Status in the sources: [EW-026] private memoir [LF-006] recorded by the monastery
- Where the sources differ: Leo XIII's 1890 decree already allowed frequent Communion; the change followed a change of chaplain (M-EW-026). The prediction is told after the event and the source is Marie (M-LF-006). Céline stresses Thérèse's own "I know nothing but what you know" (M-EW-081).
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [EW-026] Leo XIII's 1890 decree already allowed it; the change followed a change of chaplain. [LF-006] The prediction is told after the event and the source is Marie.
- Source entries: M-EW-026, M-LF-006, M-EW-081 (partial use: M-EW-081)
- Sources cited: [EW-026] CS §III "Piété eucharistique" [LF-006] LAV p.268

### M-311 — Other predictions judged fulfilled (1897–1910)
- Date: Oct 1897
- Status label: sworn at the Church process (Marie and Céline)
- What was reported: (2) Mother Agnès would be kept so busy with her she could not manage all (Marie). (3) "A harvest of young ones" in the Carmel (Céline). (4) Help to missionaries and baptism of dying children (Marie, linking to the missionary letters). (5) No incorruption: "only a skeleton", verified on 6 September 1910 (Céline). Sources: PA 7 [574], [605], [615]–[616], [624]; PA 8 [778]–[780].
- Where the sources differ: Céline denies a "true revelation" for the 1894 prediction of one sister's death (F-EW-982). Medical or natural facts: state of the remains in 1910 (skeleton).
- Source entries: M-EW-081 (partial use: M-EW-081)

### M-312 — Perfumes, lights and a sense of presence reported by the Carmel community (1897–1915)
- Date: 1897
- Status label: sworn at the Church process; listed in the Articles of the Cause (1910)
- What was reported: All the nuns "except perhaps one or two" smelled, at intervals and in various places, perfumes such as incense, roses and violets, with no natural cause. Most were sceptical at first. The perfumes began right after Thérèse's death and were more frequent in 1908–1910. Pauline smelled them about ten times; she first thought them illusions and, as prioress, made light of the reports; she prefers inner graces. Marie's first: in winter, with no flowers in the courtyard, a sweet scent of flowers near the Child Jesus statue on her way to the evening novena at the Virgin's oratory for those commending themselves to Thérèse. With Mother Agnès and another sister at work, a sudden smell of incense. Recently, after a costly act of obedience and charity, a strong scent of all kinds of flowers followed her to her cell. On the day after the death, after an act of charity, she felt Thérèse's soul come near hers with great joy (not told in 1910). Marie says she had smelled nothing for several years before the last occurrence. Several Carmelites and outsiders said they saw luminous traces in her cell or in the sky, and smelled perfumes from her objects or when they invoked her (Articles 1910, art. 105).
- The person helped: [EW-023] the community [EW-024] Marie; the community
- Witnesses and reporter: [EW-023] Mère Agnès, first-hand and from the nuns [EW-024] Marie
- Status in the sources: [EW-023] sworn testimony; not an approved miracle [EW-024] sworn testimony; not a miracle [EW-101] recorded in the Articles (1910)
- Where the sources differ: Céline says perfumes began for her only in 1908 and "had not occurred in the 11 years after the death" (see M-154).
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [EW-023] Pauline says she prefers inner graces. [EW-024] Marie says she had smelled nothing for several years before the last occurrence.
- Source entries: M-EW-023, M-EW-024, M-EW-101
- Sources cited: [EW-023] PO 1, f. 298r–v, Session 20, 3 Sept 1910 [EW-024] PA 7, [621]–[622], Session 26 [EW-101] D3 art. 105 p. 115 (PDF 129)

### M-313 — Missions in general, from 1898
- Date: 1898
- Status label: popular report / testimony at the process
- What was reported: Annals of the Propagation of the Faith recorded unhoped-for mass conversions from 1898; Fr Roulland testified to her influence in Japan, China, India; Japanese Trappistines credited vocations. Laveille PDF 408; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 451–452. Status: popular report / testimony at process.
- Source entries: M-OM-058

### M-314 — "Pluie de roses": favours reported worldwide (1899–1910)
- Date: 1899
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: Countless favours from outside the monastery: spiritual progress, conversions, healings, apparitions. Pauline published 167 accounts (1899–25 Feb 1910) under the title "Pluie de roses", with the bishop of Bayeux's imprimatur, attached to the 1909 full edition. Thousands of requests came for scraps of her clothing and objects, to obtain cures. The judges ordered the printed "Pluie de roses" and copies of recent letters added to the Acts.
- The person helped: many, unnamed
- Witnesses and reporter: Mère Agnès, from correspondence
- Status in the sources: sworn testimony; documents annexed; none approved here
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): Pauline says inside the monastery she saw "no striking cures".
- Source entries: M-EW-036
- Sources cited: PO 1, f. 299r–v, Session 20, 3 Sept 1910

### M-315 — Thérèse's influence at exorcisms (Paris, 1909–1911)
- Date: c. 1910
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: Demons, speaking through several "victims", said Thérèse helped Flamérion, freed priests from demons, and chained those demons in the "victims". A demon said: "It is your Thérèse who tore this soul from me."
- The person helped: priests (unnamed); Flamérion
- Witnesses and reporter: Flamérion
- Status in the sources: sworn testimony; Flamérion withdrew his PO deposition after the J. P. affair and re-submitted it in 1916
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): See F-EW-1927. The tribunal's editors call the J. P. affair an incident that worried Church authorities.
- Source entries: M-EW-059
- Sources cited: PO-23, f. 1178r–1179v; PA-20, p. 1106–1107

### M-316 — Missionary favours in China and the Ivory Coast (letters, 1910–1912)
- Date: 1910
- Status label: letters quoted in sworn testimony (Marie, PA 7 [622]–[624], Session 26); not miracles
- What was reported: Letters to the Carmel quoted by Marie: Fr Irénée at Wei-Hsien (China): a dispensary baptised 2,000 dying children in two months during an epidemic; the baptisers prayed to Thérèse on the road and gave her name to most baby girls. Mgr Wittner (November 1912) named "little Thérèse" coadjutrix of the vicariate of East Shantung. A superior at Mousso (Ivory Coast, April 1912) felt "an invisible hand" leading them to hidden huts of the suffering.
- Where the sources differ: Medical or natural facts: an epidemic in China. The Tabora mission letter from the same source is a separate entry.
- Source entries: M-EW-073 (partial use: M-EW-073)

### M-317 — Favours reported by the provincial of the Little Sisters of the Poor (Scotland and Ireland, 1910)
- Date: c. 1910
- Status label: sworn at the Church process
- What was reported: A nun who had suddenly left her convent came back; several old men in the homes who had given up religion for years were converted.
- The person helped: an unnamed nun; unnamed old men
- Witnesses and reporter: provincial of the Little Sisters of the Poor, in a letter to Taylor
- Status in the sources: letter quoted in sworn testimony
- Source entries: M-EW-120
- Sources cited: PO-02, f. 203r, session 13, 26 August 1910

### M-318 — The Carmel's later favours: postulants, the missions and rose petals
- Date: c. 1910
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine
- What was reported: The Carmel counts favours by the thousand in its *Pluie de roses*, including "showers of roses" (petals seen or found), perfumes and vocations; a White Father in Africa reported many conversions after her picture was placed in villages; Père Roulland reported conversions in China and Japan. (Summary entry; see F-LF-246.)
- The person helped: many
- Witnesses and reporter: letters printed by the Carmel
- Status in the sources: recorded by the shrine
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): The seven volumes were not seen.
- Source entries: M-LF-041
- Sources cited: LAV p.414–415, p.408–409; F-LF-246

### M-319 — Bullets stopped by relics, medals and pictures (First World War, 1914–1918)
- Date: c. 1916
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine
- What was reported: Relics, medals and pictures were said to have stopped or flattened bullets "beyond count"; many were sent to the Carmel with bullet marks ("Bucklers"). Laveille PDF 421–424; Interventions de Sœur Thérèse pendant la guerre; La Croix (19 and 21 Feb 1925).
- Source entries: M-OM-072, M-LF-029 (partial use: M-LF-029)

### M-320 — Thirty favours recorded on the day of the beatification (29 April 1923)
- Date: 29 Apr 1923
- Status label: recorded by the Carmel or shrine
- What was reported: [LF-034] Laveille says thirty new miracles were recorded on the very day of the beatification. [OM-084] Laveille PDF 437; Taylor 1926/30 PDF 279.
- The person helped: [LF-034] various
- Witnesses and reporter: [LF-034] Laveille (no note)
- Status in the sources: [LF-034] recorded by the shrine
- Doubts and other versions (as in the sources): [LF-034] No list in my sources.
- Source entries: M-LF-034, M-OM-084
- Sources cited: [LF-034] LAV p.437

### M-321 — Carfin, Scotland, 1923
- Date: 1923
- Status label: popular report
- What was reported: Quarter of a million pilgrims in under three months, read by Taylor as her answer to a challenge. Taylor 1926/30 PDF 433–434. Popular report.
- Source entries: M-OM-088

### M-322 — Pius XI's own favours
- Date: Jul 1925
- Status label: report of a statement by Pius XI (letter to Mgr O'Gorman, July 1925; Taylor 1926/30)
- What was reported: "Very remarkable favours … of which I have been the object" (to Mgr O'Gorman, July 1925, Taylor 1926/30 PDF 454–455); "thrown light on questions" (Laveille PDF 432).
- Source entries: M-OM-087
